回归均衡，谋定而后动 
报告导读： 
我们的观点：2027 年美股或延续内部结构再平衡，非AI 资产广度扩散有望支撑指数韧性，结构性波动仍大于指数整体波动。
AI 和通胀构成两大核心矛盾：AI 产业景气度定全年增长斜率，通胀&利率定全年交易节奏

我们的逻辑：增长斜率和流动性的时间之争：（1）分子端-2027 年美股盈利增长动能或边际放缓。结构层面：2027 年美国
AI 与非 AI 板块间的 K 型分化有望收敛，周期和科技对于美股盈利增长的贡献趋于降速，盈利动能贡献有望更均衡。（2）
分母端：宏观流动性先紧后松，AI 信用局部风险暴露。

1.  2026 年海外权益行情回顾：市场从极致 K 型分化走向结构性再平衡 
1.1   “AI 抱团-瓦解-再平衡”行情极致演绎：26 年美股更多为结构性波动，指数总体具备韧性 
回顾2026 年，美股经历了“AI 抱团-瓦解-再平衡”行情的极致演绎，概括而言，美股指数韧性强于个股、
结构波动大于总量波动。上半年，地缘冲突压制宏观风险偏好，AI 产业景气构成基本面狭窄的繁荣，市场缩圈
至业绩确定性最强的AI 硬件资产。下半年，AI 下游商业化增长动能边际放缓叠加市场对CSP 资本开支可持续
性及投资回报的质疑，前期极致抱团的AI 行情逐步瓦解，拥挤的筹码结构放大了波动。与此同时，若排除地
缘扰动的外生影响，非AI 资产亦能展现较强的韧性，实现更广泛的行业扩散，一定程度对冲了AI 板块调整的
负面影响，使得指数整体波动相对收敛。 
图 1：26 年美股更多为结构性波动，指数总体具备韧性  图 2：上半年 AI 资产贡献标普 500 上涨主要驱动，下
半年非 AI 资产再平衡对冲科技下跌对于指数的拖累

 图 4：若排除地缘扰动的外生影响，非 AI 资产亦能展
现较强的承接韧性，实现更广泛的行业扩散

数上涨驱动）

1.2  行情核心驱动：AI 产业景气趋势主导方向，抱团交易瓦解放大波动 
2026 上半年：AI 产业景气突破成为核心驱动，盈利增长消化估值压力。上半年，尽管地缘冲突扰动持续
压制宏观风险偏好，但AI Agent（Anthropic）应用场景取得突破性进展进一步强化AI 产业稀缺的景气度，
较强的盈利增长对前期估值形成消化和支撑，驱动AI 板块上涨。

2026 下半年：AI 产业潜在风险逐步显现，市场对于AI 产业叙事的预期开始松动，杀估值构成行情调整
的核心驱动，此外，资金面微观结构恶化放大波动。2026Q3 以来，市场基本完成了对于“AI 二阶导（增速）”
反转的确认，而对于“AI 一阶导（增长）”反转更多停留在预期层面质疑的加深而非现实端的确认。因此，从
DDM 拆解来看，下半年全球科技股调整更多表现为杀估值而非杀业绩。

2.  分子端：增长由高波动转向温和收敛，盈利增长降速，K 型分化收敛

总量层面：2027 年美股盈利增长动能或边际放缓。结构层面：2027 年美国AI 与非AI 板块间的 K 型分化
有望收敛，周期和科技对于美股盈利增长的贡献趋于降速，盈利动能贡献有望更均衡。具体而言：（1）科技板
块：高基数效应下，若下游商业化兑现不及预期或进展偏慢，AI 算力链周期属性强化，2027 年美股科技板块
盈利增长降速是中性假设；（ 2）能源板块：地缘和油价是核心变量，高基数效应下2027 年盈利增长趋于降
速；（3）传统板块：财政脉冲由支撑转为温和拖累，退税规模较2026 年的高位回落，房地产、传统资本开支
与低收入消费修复节奏更多取决于通胀和宏观流动性。

2.1  宏观：增长由高波动转向温和收敛 
增长由高波动转向温和收敛，通胀有望趋势回落，K 型分化收敛。2027 年美国实际GDP 季环比折年率四个
季度分别预计为2.0%、2.1%、2.1%和2.2%，全年均值约2.1%，较2026 年的2.5%回落0.4 个百分点；但季
度间振幅由2026 年的3.2 个百分点降至0.2 个百分点。2026 年的高波动源于政府停摆后的基数扰动、关税与
能源价格冲击以及AI 投资的一次性脉冲，这些因素在2027 年基本淡出，增长回到2%上下的潜在增速附近。
以四季度同比衡量，2027 年预期为2.1%，较2026 年同期的2.3%小幅回落0.2 个百分点，全年同比在2.0%—
2.3%之间，呈"中枢微降、路径平坦"的形态。驱动来看，财政脉冲由支撑转为温和拖累，退税规模较2026 年
的高位回落；房地产、传统资本开支与低收入消费受高利率压制更为明显；AI 相关投资仍在贡献增量，但边际
增速回落。 
图 13：美国主要宏观经济指标展望及美联储 9 月 SEP 展望（26Q4 后为预期值）

资料来源：国泰君安期货研究

2.1.1  中期选举后政经背景变化及对市场的影响 
展望前方，经济议题将是2027 年乃至2028 年大选年中最关键的政治博弈战场，“降低通胀”和“弥合经
济K 分化”将是持续性的政治诉求——分别对应着降低油价的天然诉求和对AI 板块的监管、再分配诉求。 
本次中期选举点出美国政经气候变化最大的特征，是选民焦点已经从意识形态的“左右”之争转为经济阶
层的“上下”之争、从文化之争转为经济之争，“K 型分化”最终清晰的显示在投票版图中，成为民主党的
Tailwind。在“由红转蓝”的部分关键摇摆州，民主党候选人以一种对“意识形态”分歧较为克制和中立的态
度，但火力全开直指降低物价等经济诉求。在过去数十年的选举周期中，“左右”之争中如移民问题、少数群
体、宗教、“美国至上”（MAGA）、去精英化等议题，在当前高物价、K 型分化、贫富分化等经济失衡加剧的背景
下再度煽动意识形态武器已然不合时宜，而共和党候选人在应对经济议题时显然无法与特朗普政府进行切割。
事实上，民主党正在获得部分MAGA 选票，2024 共和党的MAGA 群体与2026 民主党的劳动人民群体在经济逻辑
上并没有那么大差异。蓝色狂潮必然会阻碍科技和AI 进展？我们不这么认为，基本面逻辑而非政经变化仍旧
是我们前期相对看空科技板块的主因，选举只是阶段性强化这一方向。但是必须要看到“Technocracy”或者
“科技寡头-政治”的联盟已经成为当前经济背景下的对立面。

图 14：2026 年美国中期选举选民议题关注度排序

资料来源：国泰君安期货研究

落到对金融市场影响看，美国政经变化如何影响中短期市场，哪些领域政策变化概率较大、哪些领域实质
性影响不大？——我们从三类路径看可为和不可为。一是不依赖新立法、总统可自行决定：如232、301 关税
已在实际执行，对华301 条款10 月7 日确认继续有效；先进芯片出口许可与对外投资审查已有法律基础（《综
合对外投资国家安全法》）、军事行动属总统权限——无太大变化，国会能做的只有拨款附加条件、听证和诉
讼，属于降低行政执行效率的施压手段。二是需要新立法+参议院60 票+总统签署：如企业税率从21%上调至
25%—28%、恢复ACA 增强补贴与联邦医保补贴、恢复被削减的IRA 清洁能源税惠、收回总统关税授权等——民
主党推行自己的议程并非易事，对当前市场主线影响不大。普通法案在参议院需60 票才能结束辩论，推翻否
决需两院各三分之二多数。三是必须通过的法案与参议院专属权力：如12 月11 日到期的临时拨款（覆盖全部
12 项年度拨款）、已在众议院以216:212 通过的2027 财年国防授权法、延至12 月11 日的《国防生产法》授
权、2027 年债务上限；以及参议院人事确认权（美联储理事、联邦法官、内阁）与众议院传票、监督权等。—
—重要财政变化和显著债务扩张难度仍大，但我们会更关注年底债务上限博弈和政府停摆风险。

请务必阅读正文之后的免责条款部分

图 15：参议院多数立法需 60 票（预算协调除外），且总统可否决，因此民主党掌控一院或两院，主要作用是阻
止共和党议程，而非推行自己的议程

2.1.2  地缘-油价-通胀情景如何演变？通胀依赖同比基数效应

我们认为2027 年美国通胀趋势有所回落、中枢下降。但通胀趋势依赖同比基数，且近端2026Q4-2027Q1
通胀读数仍旧较高，2027Q2 同比基数效应显现。 
当前中东地缘形式总体看既保留谈判通道但局势也未实质性降温。2026 年2 月底美伊冲突爆发以来，霍
尔木兹海峡通行受伊朗方面制约，成为双方博弈的核心筹码。美国中期选举和以色列大选关键转折，美国中期
选举中民主党夺回参众两院，以色列方面内塔尼亚胡阵营失去议会多数，两者强化了冲突降温预期。外交层
面，卡塔尔、巴基斯坦持续斡旋，伊朗提出以美方解除制裁、解冻约80 亿美元资产为条件、两天内重开海峡
的“两天计划”，伊朗扬称最高领袖已下令结束“非战非和”状态、愿以谈判破局。但实质进展尚未落地，伊朗
同时表示并未与美国直接谈判，11 月以来美军对伊朗仍有“小型打击”；伊朗革命卫队解除导弹射程限制、美
军向阿曼湾货船开火；海峡接连发生油轮触雷起火与爆炸事件，沙特与胡塞武装的互袭亦未停止。 
对于原油市场而言，我们的观点是“去繁存简”，中长期仍旧视作宏观风险，一方面，主要因地缘核心矛盾
未解决，使得供给持续回归具有脆弱性（伊朗仍有袭击海峡油轮的能力、胡塞武装仍有干扰沙特原油供应能
力），原油价格持续蕴含地缘溢价，低库存水平亦支持原油估值。但另一方面，中东供给流量逐步向战前水平
恢复、运费价格有所回落、下游需求对高油价负反馈、高油价高通胀下的政治压力等也真实抑制油价上行空
间。我们按照原油75-110 美元/桶宽幅区间震荡，来对2027 年通胀水平和趋势做测算。 
我们大致测算，油价每变动10 美元/桶，2027 年CPI 同比约变动0.35 个百分点。基准情形下：75 美元
对应约1.97%，85 美元对应约2.32%，95 美元对应约2.68%，100 美元对应约2.85%，110 美元对应约
3.21%。不过，此模型仅仅考虑“油价-汽油-通胀权重-通胀”的传导路径，未能考虑1）战略储备释放、燃油
税调整等政策对冲；2）成品油裂解价差大幅走阔风险带来更强的通胀传导；3）工资—价格螺旋与通胀预期；
4）下游需求负反馈程度。 
另外，归总市场一致预期、彭博经济学家预测、市场定价和BE Model 四方预测美国CPI 同比在2026Q4-
2027Q1 维持在3.0-3.8%区间，在2027Q2 回落至2.1-2.9%区间，在2027Q3-2027Q4 回落至1.9-2.6%区

2.2  科技：年内估值下杀已基本消化 AI 产业降速的预期，2027 年更多面临现实端盈利的考验

自2025Q4 开始，“AI 交易”的风险点逐步浮出，我们大致构建了对AI 产业风险定位的框架：①一阶段
（杀估值）是在AI 下游商业化“增速”见顶放缓后对“AI 二阶导”反转的确认（对应6-8 月全球科技板块调整
的底层逻辑）；②二阶段是在中游CSP 投资回报率回落&自由现金流转负&债务融资信用风险攀升背景下对于远
期Capex 持续性预期层面质疑的加深（对应9 月以来海外股债背离加深的底层逻辑）； ③三阶段（杀业绩）是
在上游硬件价格松动、上游营收和盈利“增长”见顶回落后对于“AI 一阶导”反转的确认（尚未出现，2027
年可能逐步确认）。

展望2027 年，AI 交易的风险点随时间累积：两种情景—AI 下游商业化场景进一步兑现 OR 海外股债非对
称定价逐渐收敛。2026Q3 以来，市场基本完成了对于“AI 二阶导（增速）”反转的确认，而对于“AI 一阶导
（增长）”反转更多停留在预期层面质疑的加深而非现实端的确认。因此，从DDM 拆解来看，下半年全球科技
股调整更多表现为杀估值而非杀业绩。展望2027 年，两种情景—AI 下游商业化场景进一步兑现 OR 股债非对
称定价逐渐收敛：（ 1）乐观情景下，AI 下游实现更广义的商业化兑现（AI Agent&物理AI）—>AI 投资链实现
闭环（估值修复，业绩增长）；（2）悲观情景下，逐渐确认“AI 一阶导”反转（Capex 下修/上游硬件价格崩塌）
——>市场担忧从AI 产业增速见顶（杀估值）—>AI 产业增长见顶（杀业绩）。 
图 18：股债定价背离定位 AI 投资周期进入苛刻的下半场：25Q4 以来海外信用市场率先定价 Capex 融资持
续性与回报约束的风险，而海外权益市场定价明显隐含非对称风险

资料来源：彭博，国泰君安期货研究

2.2.1  CSP“ROIC-WACC”临界点渐近？AI 下游兑现与上游投资之间的“时差”是当前产业核心瑕疵 
北美云计算厂商ROIC-WACC 的临界点持续逼近：过去一年，北美 CSP 的ROIC-WACC 持续收敛，
Neocloud 的ROIC-WACC 几乎已全线倒挂。 
AI 上游 Capex 维持高强度&下游兑现放缓（ROIC↓）： 核心矛盾在于AI 下游需求兑现速度尚未充分匹配上
游基础设施投资扩张速度（26Q3 Anthropic ARR 放缓进一步激化矛盾）；另一方面，AI 投资下半场，前期巨
额Capex 投入对于利润的侵蚀正在逐步显性化，资产负债表对于下游需求兑现的容错率降低。 
AI 产业信用溢价走廓&宏观利率高企（WACC↑）： 一方面，信用利差走廓反映信用市场对于AI 投资风险的
重新定价：Capex 持续性与回报约束的焦虑加深&供给端恶化（科技企业大规模发债）；另一方面，地缘反复抬
高油价&通胀&海外宏观利率中枢亦间接抬升科技企业融资成本中枢。

观利率高企（WACC↑）

2.2.2  Capex 持续性的质疑是否会从预期走向现实？CSP 现金流的约束在显性化，对下游兑现容错率降低 
去年Q4 以来海外股债定价开始背离，今年9 月以来海外股债定价背离加深的底层逻辑：市场对AI 资本开
支可持续性的担忧，正在从对未来盈利与投资回报的预期博弈逐步转向对现实融资约束和资产负债表承载能力
的审视。截至2026Q3，北美 CSP 延续高强度 AI 资本开支，除微软外自由现金流均转负（需依靠外部融资维
持Capex），CSP 现金流约束正在逐步显性化，意味着后续对下游兑现容错率进一步降低。

2.2.3  Cycle 还是Super Cycle？若下游需求放缓难以证伪，AI 基建定价体系回归周期属性 
若AI 下游商业化放缓、AI 上游投资回报承压等风险持续累积且难以证伪，市场对AI 基础设施的定价逻
辑或将从超级周期（Super Cycle）逐步回归传统资本开支周期（Cycle）：2027 年AI 基建增长降速是中性假
设。历史经验，北美CSP 及费城半导体估值下修往往领先EPS 增速下修1-3 季度。 
自2025Q4 开始，“AI 交易”的风险点逐步浮出，我们大致构建了对AI 产业风险定位的框架：①一阶段
（杀估值）是在AI 下游商业化“增速”见顶放缓后对“AI 二阶导”反转的确认（对应6-8 月全球科技板块调整
的底层逻辑）；②二阶段是在中游CSP 投资回报率回落&自由现金流转负&债务融资信用风险攀升背景下对于远
期Capex 持续性预期层面质疑的加深（对应9 月以来海外股债背离加深的底层逻辑）； ③三阶段（杀业绩）是
在上游硬件价格松动、上游营收和盈利“增长”见顶回落后对于“AI 一阶导”反转的确认（尚未出现）。关
注：（1）GPU 租赁价格& HBM/DRAM 合约及现货价格；（ 2）头部LLM ARR 环比增速；（3）GPU 及服务器订单砍
单/延迟提货风险；（4）AI 融资链金融违约风险（主体评级、CDS 利差、IPO 认购倍数）；（5）Capex 预期是否
下修等维度。

2.2.4  蓝色浪潮对于 AI 产业实质性影响几何？供给端因素更多影响节奏，需求端兑现决定产业方向

蓝色浪潮一定程度强化了地方层面对AI 基础设施投资建设的政策约束。随着民主党在地方选举中取得进
展，部分州及地方政府可能加强对数据中心建设的监管，重点涉及电力消耗、环保标准、土地使用、税收优惠
及公用事业成本等方面，因此可能推高数据中心建设及运营成本，延长项目审批周期，并对新增产能的落地节
奏形成约束。 
整体而言，政策约束更多作用于AI 基础设施的供给侧，可能影响资本开支的落地节奏、建设成本及区域
布局，而AI 下游需求端兑现最终决定产业方向。一方面，相关限制措施因州而异，且多数重大政策调整仍需
经过立法、监管及行政审批程序，实际落地存在一定时滞；另一方面，已经开工或完成前期审批的项目短期受
到的影响相对有限，CSP 也可以通过调整选址、迁移项目及增加能源配套投资等方式应对。

3.  分母端：宏观流动性先紧后松，AI 信用局部风险暴露

3.1  宏观流动性：我们总体判断起点偏紧，全年先紧后松 
2027 年宏观流动性对整体宏观情绪定调有重要影响，我们总体判断起点偏紧，全年先紧后松。当前美国
金融条件已处于边际收紧态势，主要来自利率上行、美元指数走强、信用利差走阔和美股下行多种原因共振。 
美联储预计在12 月和明年3 月各加息25bp 至4.25%-4.50%，此后按兵不动。也有观点认为降息最早在
2027 年11 月启动。因此，全年大致是上半年维持偏紧、下半年视利率回落程度、股票市场和信用市场以及美
元指数从“危机模式”中走出，带动整体金融条件指数逐步转松。 
分项看2027 年宏观流动性： 
一方面看货币政策，上半年仍旧处在升息曲线中，对宏观流动性有所限制，三季度触峰回落对宏观流动性
边际贡献宽松，对加息结束的定价或在通胀显著回落或油价明显下行的可能性下重新开启。当前市场预计
2026 年12 月会议加息约1 次，此后加息预期继续累积，2027 年1 月底加息一次，隐含利率约4.27%；至3
月再度加息，隐含利率升至4.4%以上。加息定价在二季度明显放缓，2027 年7 月至9 月前后见顶，峰值隐含
利率约4.55%，相当于较当前再上行约65bp 左右。2027 三季度后，曲线小幅回落，至2027 年11 月至12 月
隐含利率回落至4.45%左右。这说明市场预期本轮加息在2027 年年中结束，下半年开始计入小幅降息，但全
年政策利率仍明显高于当前水平。 
另一方面看金融条件如股票市场、信用市场和美元指数，整体上看，当前我们认为股票市场调整在中期尚
未结束，而信用市场尤其是科技板块自由现金流恶化预计延续至2027 年中，信用利差或对金融条件指数构成
拖累，美元指数跟随利差逻辑和市场风偏较难判断，但预计美元指数在2027Q1 维持相对强势，对金融条件构
成抑制，而后回落对边际改善金融条件。

3.2  信用风险：AI 产业融资循环链条局部信用风险值得动态关注 
3.2.1  AI 基建投资正在明显增加产业链相关公司对债务融资的依赖 
据IMF 数据：北美云服务商在全球投资级债券发行中的份额从2024 年的不足1%升至2026 年的约10%。
虽然其核心业务盈利强劲，但持续高额的Capex 投资使得FCF 快速下降（除微软外均已转负），AI 基建投资正
在明显增加对债务融资的依赖。而Neocloud 等企业通常负债更高、盈利能力更弱，可能成为AI 生态体系中的
薄弱环节。

3.2.2  AI 循环融资系统（Circular Financing）加大了债务、股权和未来收入之间的相关性

据《Urbanomics ：Update on the AI spending boom》分析：AI 融资体系可能存在三类相互重叠的
风险——循环融资（Circular Financing）、供应商融资（Vendor Financing）和表外杠杆（Off-
balance-sheet Leverage）。它们并非相互独立的融资交易，而是横跨七类产业参与者、相互关联的信用风
险结构。 
从融资机制看，AI 产业正在形成资金循环，使得原本相互独立的双边交易，演变为具有自我强化机制的融
资体系。芯片厂商及Hyperscalers 向AI Lab 提供股权投资，AI Lab 随后将获得的资金转化为对芯片厂商和
云服务商的采购承诺，这些采购承诺进一步形成供应商的在手订单（Backlog）并支撑其债券融资；与此同
时，金融机构通过SPV 和私人信贷为整个AI 基础设施建设提供资金。 
AI 融资体系可能存在三类相互重叠的风险：（1）AI 循环融资可能放大表观需求。（2）关键主体的风险可
能沿AI 产业链同步传导，原本分散在不同环节的风险实际上依赖于同一底层需求假设。如果OpenAI 无法履行
相关承诺，Oracle/CoreWeave 的在手订单、NVIDIA 与AMD 的销售预期，以及SPV 贷款人的抵押品价值，都
可能同时受到重新定价。（3）风险可能向透明度较低的融资渠道转移（私人信贷和SPV 等结构），近两年私人
信贷市场的杠杆与流动性风险持续上升。 
总体而言，只有当AI 需求不及预期时，相关风险才可能集中显现，但由于七类参与者实际上共同承担对
AI 需求增长的押注，一旦需求明显放缓，相关风险可能在多个环节同步暴露，潜在冲击可能通过企业、金融机
构及居民资产负债表向金融市场和实体经济扩散。

3.2.3  2027 年尚未出现全面信用收缩的典型特征，但AI 链条局部信用风险值得动态关注

展望2027 年：美国尚未出现全面信用收缩的典型特征，但AI 链条局部信用及流动性脆弱性有所累积。当
前，美国银行体系资本充足率仍接近历史较高水平，企业与居民债务占GDP 比重持续回落，整体债务脆弱性及
信用风险相对可控，尚未出现全面信用收缩的典型特征，但AI 产业链条循环融资体系内结构性风险值得关
注。

 25 
4.  美股结论与投资展望：增配价值，回归均衡，谋定而后动

市场判断：2027 年美股或延续内部结构再平衡，非AI 资产广度扩散有望支撑指数韧性，结构性波动仍大
于指数整体波动，AI 产业景气度定全年增长弹性，通胀&利率定全年交易节奏。 
战略层面：增配价值与防御，回归均衡配置。我们去年以来持续提示的AI 产业四阶段框架，2027 年大致
处于第二阶段向第三阶段逐步过渡的窗口：建议降低AI 硬件，增配必选消费、医疗保健、公用事业等低AI 相
关性资产，回归盈利质量与现金流稳定性增强组合韧性。更长期的视角（第四阶段）， AI 下游（CSP/应用/软
件/物理AI 等）将成为主线资产（目前仍然处于左侧观察区间）。 
战术层面：顺势博弈通胀缓和与利率回落驱动的修复行情。若通胀改善、利率中枢下行且经济保持韧性，
金融条件改善有望催化房地产、可选消费等传统型利率敏感资产+医疗保健代表的成长型利率敏感资产的阶段性
反弹。

资料来源：国泰君安期货研究

### Page 7

MPI (6223.TWO)
26 November 2026 Citi Research
7
MPI
Company description
Founded in 1995 in Hsinchu, Taiwan, MPI Corporation  aims to build precision
probing system for the Taiwan semiconductor testing market. In its early
years, the company focused primarily on engineering probers for laboratory
and wafer-level characterization. By early 2000s, MPI had leveraged its
precision machinal base to move up the value chain into vertical probe cards,
targeting the testing of DRAM and logic devices. MPI also established its in-

house PCB processing and substrate assembly lines to ensure quality control
and shorten turnaround time. Over time, MPI has become a key local partner
for leading OSATs, supporting vertical probe cards used for volume
production in memory and SoC testing. With development in MEMS probe-
card fabrication for sub-50um pitches, MPI has developed proprietary
process for micro-spring and thin-film formation which has transformed MPI
from a precision-mechanical manufacturer into a micro-electromechanical

device company - directly competing with global leaders like FormFactor and
Technophobe in the most technology-intensive segment of the testing
interface market.
Investment strategy
We have a Buy/High Risk rating on MPI as we view it as a key beneficiary of the
increasing demand for and complexity of probe cards, with AI chips migrating
to more advanced node with chiplet design and bigger die sizes. We have seen
the company transitioning from a cyclical probe card supplier to a structural

beneficiary of AI-driven test complexity. With  promising growth potential
from AI chips, we expect MPI to deliver sustainable earnings growth and an
improving sales mix, supported by its rising competitive moats.
Valuation
Our target price for MPI is set at NT$5,500, based on a target PE multiple of
35x applied to our 2027-28E EPS average. We believe our PE target, in line
with MPI's 3-year forward PE range, is justified given MPI’s earnings power

remains structurally higher than that in the previous cycles, broadening
demand across AI ASIC, additional CPU opportunities and non-AI ASIC wins.
We expect MPI to see accelerating earnings growth ahead supported by
sustainable sales growth and an improving mix.
Risks
Our quant risk rating system rates the MPI stock as High Risk for its share-
price volatility. Key downside risks that could prevent MPI shares from
reaching our target price include 1) slowdown in AI ASIC demand; 2) slower-

than-expected adoption of MEMS probe cards; 3) intensifying competition
from global and regional peers; 4) slow development of the CPO equipment
business.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

### Page 3

WinWay Technology (6515.TW)
26 November 2026 Citi Research
3
WinWay Technology
Valuation
We value WinWay using a PE-based approach that factors in earnings momentum and likely expansion of its addressable
market. Our target price of NT$6,500 is derived from applying c.30x to 2H27E-1H28E EPS, in line with its historical average PE
since 2023, which we believe is justified given the company's entrenched position in the high-end AI GPU testing supply chain,

rising contribution from coaxial socket and SLT solutions, and the increasing value of test content per module. With these
structural drivers in place, and as we believe the market is starting to value it as an AI/HPC play (rather than a consumer
electronics stock), we expect the multiple to hold at a premium relative to its historical averages.
Risks
Our quant rating system rates the WinWay stock High Risk based on its historical volatility.
Key downside risks that could impede the stock from reaching our target price and our earnings forecasts include: 1) client and

sector concentration – specifically any delays in US AI GPU product roadmap could impact the business outlook for WinWay; 2)
intensifying competition in coaxial and hypersockets from global peers; 3) faster-than-expected testing algorithm
optimization, which could shorten testing durations and reduce the anticipated hardware demand multiplier; and 4) delay of
MEMS Phase 2 mass production.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds

33.94%，符合先前自結獲利公告狀況，營益率 22.12%，略優於預
期，稅前淨利 32.52 億元，歸屬母公司稅後淨利 23.88 億元，單季
稅後 EPS 4.60 元，符合預期。 
◼ 2027 年中開出 10M 新產能將瞄準高階產品：目前景碩 ABF 稼動率
達 95%，產能近滿載，預期 2027 年景碩在 Vera CPU 市佔率維持
70%，新增產能預計主攻 GPU 產品，目標取得 Rubin 產品 30%市
佔率，並積極爭取 AMD Venice、MI450 及 Google TPU 等訂單。隨
台廠高階載板技術逐步成熟，且日系載板廠產能滿載，預期 NV 及
AMD 將 持 續 提 高 台 廠 採 購 比 重 。 產 能 方 面 ，K6A 廠 已 量 產 ，

工，支應中長期需求。原物料方面，近期 T-glass 供應已逐步舒緩，
係因 Nittobo 新增了 30%產能，亦導入台廠 T-glass 並持續推動
Hybrid 方案。

景碩為華碩轉投資 IC 載板廠  景碩科技成立於 2000 年，資本額 52 億元，為華碩集團旗下轉投資 IC 載板廠
商，主要生產各種積體電路用球型柵狀陣列(BGA)基板，公司發展策略著重少
量多樣利基型 BGA 基板；總公司位於桃園新屋，主要生產基地位於台灣(桃園
新屋、新竹新豐、桃園楊梅)以及中國(江蘇蘇州)。

景碩 2025 年產品營收佔比：Substrate 82%(ABF 46%+BT36%)、隱形眼鏡(晶
碩)18%。預估 2026 年 Substrate 佔比提升至 85%，+43%YOY，其中 ABF 受
惠 AI 晶片，+44%YOY，佔比提升至 48%，隱形眼鏡佔比 15%，+16%YOY；
預估 2027 年 Substrate 佔比提升至 89%，其中 ABF 佔比顯著提升至 57%，
+83%YOY。

股價相對大盤走勢

達興材料(5234 TT) Trading Buy

半導體材料持續推升年營收成長，評等為 Trading Buy。  
投資建議：雖然台灣面板廠關閉部份產線將影響顯示器材料出
貨表現，不過，預估半導體材料出貨規模將因台灣晶圓代工廠
提高在地供應比重而持續擴大，2027 年營收展望優於 2026 年，
故群益對達興材的投資建議為 Trading Buy。  
3Q26 稅後淨利 2.35 億元，QoQ+2.67%：由於半導體材料出貨
規模擴大，3Q26 營收 13.82 億元，QoQ+7.82%。雖然受到部份
顯示器材料來料異常影響而提列存貨跌價損失，毛利率降低至
41.25%，但在營收規模擴大下，營業利益增加至 2.74 億元，
QoQ+6.60%，稅後淨利 2.35 億元，QoQ+2.67%。  
2027 年半導體材料營收展望佳：達興材 2027 年的營收動能仍
來自半導體材料，達興材除自行開發產品外，亦與國際材料大
廠策略合作，1H26 半導體材料已量產的產品為 13 項，帶動
1Q~3Q26 半導體材料及關鍵原材料營收增加至 9.73 億元，
YoY+52.03%。其中，先進製程重要產品包括自行開發的乾蝕刻
後清洗液，以及 2 項 2 奈米製程材料(1 項為自行開發，1 項為
與國際材料大廠策略合作開發)；成熟製程重要產品為與國際材
料大廠合作開發的光阻用高純度高分子；至於先進封裝重要產
品則為自行開發的雷射離型層、高選擇比銅蝕刻液。由於半導
體材料 2H26 量產產品再新增 5 項，且達興材指出將自 2027 年
開始貢獻營收，搭配中港 2 廠新產能開出，預估半導體材料營
收 YoY+38.25%。  
再就長線出貨展望而言，由於台灣晶圓代工大廠預計在 2030 年
以前將在地採購比重提高至 68%，此一趨勢有利於達興材新產
品打入相關供應鏈。目前達興材半導體材料已送樣至客戶處認
證的產品共 14 項，並另有 8 項產品處於開發階段，均為 2028
年至 2029 年潛在營收動能，達興材並規劃在 2030 年以前將半
導體材料的營收比重由 1Q~3Q26 的 23%提高至 30%。

2 群益投顧 
  企業 ESG 資訊揭露： 
就溫室氣體排放而言，達興材相關管理策略主要有三，(1)組織面碳管理，每
年依據 ISO14064-1 標準執行溫室氣體盤查與查證，並依循國際標準逐步增
加盤查類別，提高碳資訊揭露透明度，作為推動節能減碳相關工作之基礎；
(2)產品面碳管理，依據 ISO14067 標準執行主要產品碳盤查，掌握產品單位
能耗及碳排放量，逐步建置內部產品碳足跡資料庫；(3)系統面碳管理，建置
內部碳管理平台，將溫室氣體相關電子表單進行系統化管理，以做為定期檢
討營運碳排的管理手段。 
再就廢棄物管理政策或減量目標而言，達興材積極推動廢棄物回收及資源
化，透過容器回收、溶劑回收以及能源回收來達到減量，相關目標主要有二，
(1)廢溶劑回收率(含能源回收)目標為 90%； (2)洗釜溶劑重複利用率目標為
80%。  
雖半導體材料出貨規模擴大，但顯示器來料問題影響毛利率表現，3Q26 稅
後淨利 2.35 億元，QoQ+2.67%：  
達興材為顯示器材料廠，為友達(2409 TT)、長興(1717 TT)合資設置，產品
包括彩色濾光片(color filter)感光間隙材料(全球市佔率第 1)、液晶 (cell)配向
膜、array 銅蝕刻液(主打高階產品市場，在 8K 面板維持領先地位)等，並配
合友達集團跨足巨量轉移暫時接著膠、白色光阻等 Micro LED 面板材料領
域，近幾年合計營收比重均超過 80%。  
此外，達興材亦進軍半導體材料(晶圓級/面板級先進封裝、先進/成熟製程材
料 )、綠能(電動車電池、高階工具機電池材料)市場，並以半導體材料出貨潛
力較佳。首先，在先進封裝材料的部份，產品除包括有機離型層、光阻剝離
液、高選擇比銅蝕刻液等間接材外，亦佈局高解析感光介電材、低溫低介電
感光介電材、耐高溫接著膠等直接材(永久材)；其次，在晶圓製程材料的部
份，產品則包括特用高純度溶劑(成熟製程，供維護、清洗高階管線之用)、
光阻剝離液(成熟製程)、高選擇比銅蝕刻液(先進製程)、高純度高分子(成熟
製程)、底部抗反射層剝離液(成熟製程)等。 
達興材擁有中科廠、中港 1 廠、2 廠，達興材賴以起家的面板材料生產機台
係設置於中科廠、中港 1 廠，至於近期出貨規模持續擴大的半導體材料除少
數機台設置於中科廠、中港 1 廠外，後續新機台則均規畫設置於中港 2 廠
(半導體材料所需之無塵室等級通常高於面板材料)，該廠為 4 層樓建築，目
前 1 樓設置 10 條生產線(均已量產)， 2 樓則規畫於 2027 年以前再陸續建置
10 條生產線，此外，達興材尚規劃配合客戶需求於高雄橋頭設廠，預計 2027
年動工、4Q28 完工，待完工後可就近供貨(最快 2029 年開始貢獻營收)。  
3Q26 營收 13.82 億元，QoQ+7.82%，動能來自半導體材料及關鍵原材料，
相關營收 4.02 億元，QoQ+25.87%，雖然受到部份顯示器材料來料異常影響
而提列存貨跌價損失(影響毛利率 2~2.5%)，毛利率降低至 41.25%(2Q26 為
42.91%)，但在營收規模擴大下，營業利益增加至 2.74 億元，QoQ+6.60%，
稅後淨利 2.35 億元，QoQ+2.67%。  
受惠於新產品、新產能效應，2027 年半導體材料營收展望佳： 
達興材 2027 年的營收動能仍來自半導體材料，達興材除自行開發產品外，

### Page 3

個股報告 
                                        2026 年 11 月 17 日

3 群益投顧 
亦與國際材料大廠策略合作，1H26 半導體材料已量產的產品為 13 項，帶動
1Q~3Q26 半導體材料及關鍵原材料營收增加至 9.73 億元，YoY+52.03%。其
中，先進製程重要產品包括自行開發的乾蝕刻後清洗液，以及 2 項 2 奈米製
程材料(1 項為自行開發，1 項為與國際材料大廠策略合作開發)；成熟製程
重要產品為與國際材料大廠合作開發的光阻用高純度高分子；至於先進封
裝重要產品則為自行開發的雷射離型層、高選擇比銅蝕刻液。由於半導體材
料 2H26 量產產品再新增 5 項，且達興材指出將自 2027 年開始貢獻營收，
搭配中港 2 廠新產能開出，預估半導體材料營收 YoY+38.25%。  
再就長線出貨展望而言，由於台灣晶圓代工大廠預計在 2030 年以前將在地
採購比重提高至 68%，此一趨勢有利於達興材新產品打入相關供應鏈。目前
達興材半導體材料已送樣至客戶處認證的產品共 14 項，並另有 8 項產品處
於開發階段，均為 2028 年至 2029 年潛在營收動能，達興材並規劃在 2030
年以前將半導體材料的營收比重由 1Q~3Q26 的 23%提高至 30%。  
此外，在顯示器材料部份，雖然台灣面板廠關閉部份產線的影響程度將自
2H27 開始擴大，不過，達興材已積極拓展中國市場，預估 2027 年顯示器材
料營收仍得以維持與 2026 年接近的水準。 
考量半導體材料推升 2027 年營收持續成長，群益對達興材的投資建議為
Trading Buy：  
雖然台灣面板廠關閉部份產線將影響顯示器材料出貨表現，不過，預估半導
體材料出貨規模將因台灣晶圓代工廠提高在地供應比重而持續擴大，2027
年營收展望優於 2026 年，故群益對達興材的投資建議為 Trading Buy。

  【投資評等說明】 
  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15%≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<15%

中立(Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等 
預期近期股價將處於盤整 
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】 
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。  
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。 
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」、「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。 
停止推薦情境： 
1. 達目標價。 
2. 雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。 
3. 推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】 
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

7717 萊德光電-KY 
(Lightel Co.)

報告日期：2026/11/25 研 究 員：方姿婷 (Lesley Fang) 
壹、投 資 建 議 
重點摘要： 
 萊德光電 3Q26 營收為 3.38 億元，+1.50%QoQ、+65.69%YoY，
毛利率為 68.59%，單季稅後 EPS+2.33 元，3Q26 毛利率優於預
期，受惠於衛星雷射通訊帶動。 
 營運成長來自低軌衛星/光通訊需求，2027 年 LCT(光放大器及
其零組件)用量於衛星通訊 2-4 顆搭載量，在 AI Data Center 建置
將提升數量至 6 顆，並需要提升光放大器級數，呈現倍數增長。 
 展望 2027 年，衛星通訊 SpaceX Gen3(V3)改版升級，帶動萊德
光電與低軌衛星產業營運動能呈倍數成長。

一. 3Q26 營收與財報優於預期，4Q26 毛利率維持高檔 
萊德光電 3Q26 營收為 3.38 億元，+1.50%QoQ、+65.69%YoY，毛利
率為 68.59%，單季稅後 EPS+2.33 元，3Q26 毛利率優於預期，受惠
於衛星雷射通訊帶動。展望 4Q26，預估萊德光電營收為 3.87 億元，
+14.50%QoQ，+110.33%YoY，毛利率為 63.44%，單季稅後 EPS+2.65
元，預估萊德光電 2026 年全年 EPS+9.32 元。

二. 2H26 起萊德光電受惠低軌衛星與光通訊帶動成長 
宏遠投顧觀察，低軌衛星產業於 2H26 起，適逢低軌衛星客戶設計
變更，根據 SpaceX 在申請文件中指出，SpaceX Gen3(V3)衛星重量
將大幅增加至最高 2,000 公斤，遠高於目前 Gen2 衛星的乘載重量
約 575 公斤。V3 火箭新平台採用更大的衛星主體、先進相控陣天
線、更長的太陽能板、新一代機載電腦與數據機等，低軌衛星受
惠於 SpaceX V2mini 火箭轉型升級至 V3 版本新型火箭對於低軌衛
星乘載量增，帶動低軌衛星營收動能於 2H26 緩步爬坡增長，萊德
光電於座談會中指出 V3 版本新型火箭搭載低軌衛星數量高達 60
顆，為原本的 V2 版本火箭搭載數量之 3 倍。

三. 萊德光電於低軌衛星雷射，具備專業製程與供貨 
萊德光電於 3Q26(2026.11.24)座談會中提及，太空經濟商機市場規
模以 Space X 為例，評估為衛星通訊之 10 倍量體(評估由 1.3 萬顆
增至 10-15 萬顆)。涵蓋 Google、 NVIDIA 及其他美系 CSP 業者談
及太空資料中心，隨著頻寬需求提升，需要建設光基地站進行太
空與地表之間光纖通訊傳輸。衛星光通訊需求，2027 年 LCT(光放
投資建議：買進 
目標價位：635.00 元

2 
大器當中的 Filter/Isolator 以及 Pump/Signal Combiner)用量於衛星通
訊 2-4 顆搭載量，在 AI Data Center 建置將提升數量至 6 顆，並且
需要提升光放大器之級數，呈現倍數增長；除了搭載數量本身之
增長，LCT 光放大器更會因應光功率升級，LCT 會從 2W 升級至
5W/10W 對此結構設計級數會提高、多數時候 LCT 元件之瓦數愈
高單價也可能同步提升。

四.預估 2026/2027 年稅後 EPS 9.32/13.80 元 
2026 年~2027 年營運動能來自於低軌衛星產業規格升級、太空 AI
資料中心建置所需(衛星之間光纖通訊傳輸量增)，隨著頻寬需求提
升，需要建設光基地站進行太空與地表之間光纖通訊傳輸。預估
萊德光電 2026 年營收為 13.15 億元，+52.55%YoY，毛利率為
63.74%，稅後淨利為 2.31 億元，稅後 EPS+9.32 元。展望 2027 年，
隨著產業需求端成長帶動，預估萊德光電營收為 17.34 億元，
+31.86%YoY，毛利率為 63.98%，稅後淨利為 3.43 億元，稅後
EPS+13.80 元。

四.投資建議為買進，目標價 635 元 
宏遠投顧認為，2026 年~2027 年基於光通訊產業、衛星通訊產業需
求因應 AI 資料中心建置，所需要之衛星通訊 LCT(光放大器)用量
增長，及其他相關零組件(Filter/Isolator/ Signal Combiner)商機成長
可期。基於上述，給予萊德光電投資建議為買進，目標價 635 元
(46.00x 2027 EPS)。

貳、公 司 概 況

一. 公司簡介 
萊德光電(7717)為光纖通訊內，高階光纖被動元件製造商，主要生
產光纖通訊被動元件、光纖雷射被動元件、衛星通訊雷射被動元
件(光纖隔離器、合數器等)、光纖端面檢測儀與製造設備等，產品
用於衛星間通訊雷射(ISL)與終端(LCT)。萊德光電產品組合中，衛
星雷射通訊被動元件 57%、其他佔 43%。

二.2026~2027 年營運概況及展望 
1.萊德光電受惠低軌衛星發展，光通訊 LD 被動元件搭載數量增長 
依據低軌衛星產業動向，截至 2026 年 SpaceX 有 9,600 顆 Starlink
寬頻與 Mobile 衛星，在低軌道運行，Starlin 服務用戶數達 1,030
萬戶，衛星規格 Starlink 逐步由 V2 mini 過渡至 V3，新一代 V3 衛
星下行頻寬 1Tbps、上行頻寬 160Gbps，衛星之間通訊達 4Tbps，
可使得衛星間傳輸資料速率提升，同時提升網絡覆蓋與服務品質。 
衛星之間通訊之強化，會成為穩懋衛星營收增漲之關鍵。

此外，太空資料中心進程蔚為產業趨勢，衛星數量增加，因應衛
星持續入軌，發射瓶頸逐漸克服；低軌衛星及 AI Data Center 的增
量可期。隨著低軌衛星量體增長，LCT(光放大器)數量增，Typical：
低軌衛星 LCT 3-4 顆；AI Data Center LCT 6 顆。長遠因應頻寬頻
寬需求提升，對於光功率增大，對應光放大器的級數增加，搭載
之光元件數量亦倍增。客戶訂單加速，預期低軌衛星產業未來 5
年火箭發射頻率及運量將雙雙增加。

Document 267: AI都攻克黎曼猜想了，为什么机器人还拧不好一颗螺丝？

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI都攻克黎曼猜想了，为什么机器人还拧不好一颗螺丝？.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 6
Status: ok

### Page 1

具⾝智能的瓶颈正从⼤脑理解与动作规划，转向真实物理世界中的精细操作。机器⼈不仅
缺乏对接触⼒、摩擦和滑动的感知能⼒，灵巧⼿与机械臂之间的协同、操作技能的训练与
复⽤也尚未成熟。如何打通感知、执⾏与学习，让机器⼈稳定完成复杂操作，成为⾏业规
模化落地的关键。
摘要：
AI都能破解黎曼猜想这种百年数学难题、证明⼤名⿍⿍的代数版P≠NP了，可真让AI指挥机器⼈
叠件⾐服、收拾⼀桌杂物，或者做顿饭，它的动作还是太费劲了。
把⽬光转向⽣产线，机器⼈也很难⼲好⼤量看似简单的⼯作。
汽⻋⼯⼚⾥的线束插拔、电池包装配，家电产线中的精密零部件组装，看起来⽆⾮是对准、握
住、推进⼏步，却需要机器⼈持续感知接触状态、调整⼒度和姿态。
现在，具⾝智能的⼀⼤瓶颈就在于，⼤脑发展⻜快，机器⼈越来越会理解指令、规划动作，却
很难稳定完成精细操作。它需要更灵敏的感知，也需要⼀整套把感知变成操作的能⼒。
今天，⼀⽬科技宣布战略升级，基于触觉感知构建机器⼈灵巧操作⽣态（YIMU xEco ——
Dexterous Manipulation Ecosystem）。

此次升级围绕四层产品能⼒：
⾼维感知：把触觉传感器的感知能⼒进⼀步开发、释放，这是物理世界的⼊⼝；
灵巧操作：把灵巧⼿、机械臂，做成⼀个完整的、可交付的上肢本体；
技能沉淀：⼩脑模型将原⼦动作组合成有⾃适应能⼒的Skill。原⼦动作解决复⽤，Skill解决
任务封装；
开放部署：把“⼈类操作演⽰—仿真建模训练—真机强化学习—部署应⽤”这条链路，打磨成⼀
套开发者⾃⼰能上⼿的⼯具，开放给所有客⼾。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

早在2023年，⼀⽬科技就押注⼀个当时⼏乎没⼈相信的命题——要让机器⼈拥有像⼈⼀样的触
觉。如今，这家公司为什么要从⼀触觉感知出发，进⼀步构建⼀套完整的机器⼈灵巧操作⽣
态？
答案就在于，触觉只是机器⼈感知物理世界的⼊⼝，真正实现灵巧操作，还需要打通上肢硬件
集成、操作技能沉淀与场景开发部署等多个环节，让感知能⼒转化为可执⾏、可复⽤、可规模
化交付的操作能⼒。
机器⼈精细操作，为什么还⼀直卡在⼿感上？
⼈类的⼀双⼿，到底有多神奇？
拧瓶盖、插充电线、叠⾐服，这些动作我们⼏乎不⽤思考就能完成。可对机器⼈来说，轻轻⼀
握、稍微⼀转，想要把握好度，都太难了！
如今，机器⼈能跑能跳，⼤脑也越来越聪明，但⾯对真实场景中的精细操作，依然显得笨⼿笨
脚。⼈类习以为常的⼿感，恰恰是机器⼈尚未真正掌握的能⼒。
以插⼊⼀个接头为例，眼睛能够帮助我们找到接⼝，但真正插进去，还得靠⼿指调整⼒度。碰
到阻⼒，我们会下意识地换个⻆度、轻轻再推。机器⼈主要依靠视觉判断位置，接触部位却容
易被遮挡，摄像头也难以直接感知接触⼒、摩擦⼒和滑动趋势。⼈与机器⼈之间的操作差距，

就藏在⼿指调整的那⼏毫⽶⾥。
这也是触觉的重要性所在，机器⼈需要通过触觉获得接触反馈，才能建⽴“接触—反馈—调整”的
操作闭环。
但给机器⼈装上⼀双有感觉的⼿，难度远超想象。⽬前⾏业存在压阻式、电容式、光学式等多
种触觉技术路线，视触觉凭借⾼分辨率受到关注。
但指尖空间有限，传感器既要⾜够灵敏，⼜要耐受频繁挤压和摩擦，还得兼顾⼩型化、响应速
度与成本。⼀双好⽤的机器⼿，既要感知细微变化，也得经受真实⼯作的磨损。
硬件之外，⼿感该怎么教给机器⼈，同样是难题。触觉数据需要通过真实接触采集，不同传感
器的数据格式缺乏统⼀标准，仿真⼜难以完整还原复杂摩擦和柔性材料形变。机器⼈想掌握更
多精细操作，仍需要积累⼤量真实物理交互经验。
围绕这些难题，⼀⽬科技已在触觉领域深耕多年。

### Page 3

其核⼼产品视触觉传感器，通过柔性材料接触物体后的形变与光影变化，利⽤内部光学器件采
集信息，再经算法转化为压⼒分布、纹理和滑动趋势等物理信号，让机器⼈能够感知原本难以
直接观察的接触状态。
据⼀⽬科技披露，⾃研的F20、F80等触觉传感器已实现量产出货，产品经过508万次循环压
测，出⼚⼀致性达到0.02N量级。相关解决⽅案已进⼊医疗科研、汽⻋及家电装配等场景，涉及
±0.1g微⼩固体试剂称量、线缆装配及重复性化验等任务。
指尖有感觉后，机器⼈还需要⼀套完整的硬件体系
端起⼀杯⽔，这个再简单不过了。可仔细想想，⼿指要握得稳，⼿腕要控制姿态，胳膊还得平
稳移动，哪个环节配合不好，⽔就可能洒出来，机器⼈也是如此。
给指尖装上触觉传感器，只是让它有了感觉；想真正⼲好活，还得让整条胳膊配合起来。抓
取、插拔、装配等精细任务，都需要灵巧⼿、机械臂、关节和执⾏器共同完成。
可在现实中，这套配合远没有想象中容易。⽬前，触觉传感器、灵巧⼿和机械臂往往由不同⼚
商提供。客⼾好不容易采购⻬了零部件，装到⼀起才发现，还需要做结构、电⽓、算法三层适

配。机器⼈还没开始⼲活，⼯程师可能已经忙得不可开交。
更⿇烦的是，单个零部件表现出⾊，拼起来也未必好⽤。⼀双灵敏的⼿，加上⼀条有⼒的胳
膊，未必就能组成⼀套灵巧的操作系统。不同部件之间的协同表现，直接影响整套设备的操作
精度、稳定性和量产交付能⼒。
所以，⼀⽬科技将产品布局从触觉感知延伸⾄完整的上肢执⾏系统，把机器⼈上肢作为完整产
品定义、标定和交付。
⼀⽬科技将指尖⾼维接触感知、灵巧⼿、机械臂、关节和执⾏器整合起来，统⼀考虑结构设
计、接⼝适配和系统标定，希望交到客⼾⼿上的，是⼀套能够更快进⼊真实⼯位、真正执⾏精
细操作的机器⼈上肢。
对客⼾⽽⾔，少⼀些复杂的硬件集成，才有更多精⼒解决⽣产现场的实际问题。
据了解，⼀⽬科技⾸款全掌触觉灵巧⼿YIMU xHand计划在2027年CES亮相。相⽐仅在指尖布
置触觉感知，全掌触觉有望让机器⼈进⼀步感知⼿掌与物体之间的接触状态，为更复杂的抓握
和⼿内操作提供信息。

### Page 4

像搭积⽊⼀样，把操作技能变成可复⽤的智能资产
触觉让机器⼈有了⼿感，完整的上肢让它能够执⾏动作，但距离真正⼲好活，还差⼀个关键环
节：机器⼈得学会如何根据接触反馈，决定下⼀步该怎么动。
⼈⼲活久了会积累⼿感和经验，碰到问题⾃然知道如何调整，机器⼈同样需要让感知、控制模
型与执⾏系统配合起来。
以机器⼈安装灯泡为例，它需要控制机械臂的位置、灵巧⼿的姿态和指尖接触⼒，先稳稳握住
灯泡，再对准灯座，旋转过程中还要根据接触阻⼒不断调整⼒度和⻆度，避免拧偏、打滑或⽤
⼒过猛。
这些⼈类⼏秒钟就能完成的动作，背后可能需要机器⼈反复训练，才能把每⼀步衔接起来。
更让⼈头疼的是，机器⼈好不容易学会了⼀⻔⼿艺，换个地⽅可能就⼜得从头学起。 换⼀种零
件、调整⼀下⻆度，甚⾄更换机械臂和灵巧⼿，原先学会的技能都可能需要重新采集数据、训
练和调试。
这暴露出具⾝智能规模化落地的⼀道难题：机器⼈需要积累的，是可以反复使⽤的本领，⽽⾮
只能完成⼀次的任务。怎么将操作经验抽象为标准化技能，让机器⼈能够跨任务调⽤和组合，

是⾏业亟需解决的关键问题。
因此，⼀⽬科技选择⽤⼩脑模型将原⼦动作组合成有⾃适应能⼒的Skill，试图解决技能复⽤问
题。
这个思路有点像打格⽃游戏：单独学会出拳、闪避只是基础，真正厉害的是把这些动作组合成
⼀套套连招。
原⼦动作负责沉淀可复⽤的基础能⼒，Skill则将相关动作封装成完整任务，让机器⼈⾯对不同
需求时，有机会直接调⽤已有的底层能⼒。
这样⼀来，机器⼈⾟苦练出的操作经验，就有机会成为可调⽤、可组合的智能资产，在更多地
⽅派上⽤场，减少新任务的重复开发。
让更多⼈来教机器⼈⼲活，激活灵巧操作的产业创造⼒
归根结底，想教机器⼈学会⼀项新技能，依然是件很不容易的事。

### Page 5

开发者需要先采集数据，再搭建仿真环境、训练模型，最后还要在真实机器⼈上反复调试，才
能让它真正⼲活。这⼀过程涉及多套⼯具，操作复杂、耗时费⼒，也让不少开发者望⽽却步。
对于企业来说，每让机器⼈学会⼀项新⼯作，都可能意味着新⼀轮的开发投⼊。
因此，⾏业迫切需要⼀套更简单、易上⼿的开发⼯具，让更多⼈能够参与进来。
为了改变这种局⾯，⼀⽬科技希望通过YIMU xStudio，将“⼈类操作演⽰—仿真建模训练—真
机强化学习—部署应⽤”串成⼀套标准化⼯具链。原本需要跨越多套系统的开发过程，有望变得
更加连贯、易⽤。
这套⼯具链想解决的，还有⼤量重复劳动。
假如⼀名开发者已经教会机器⼈完成某种操作，另⼀名开发者⾯对相近任务，就有机会调⽤已
有Skill，再根据实际需要调整、组合，让开发者少花时间重复造轮⼦，把更多精⼒留给真正需要
解决的现场问题。
据了解，⼀⽬科技还规划了社区与云服务平台YIMU xWorld，⽀持设备连接和远程操作，希望
吸引更多开发者参与。
未来，开发者可以围绕具体需求创造、开放和共享Skill，让不同团队积累的操作经验，有机会被

更多客⼾使⽤，从⽽获得商业价值，逐步形成技能持续丰富、应⽤不断扩展的⻜轮。
结语：操作能⼒，成为具⾝智能竞争的新分⽔岭
具⾝⼤脑与机器⼈本体不断进步之后，能否在复杂真实环境中稳定完成精细操作，将进⼀步决
定技术落地的深度与⼴度。
这也是⼀⽬科技押注灵巧操作⽣态的原因。通过打通触觉感知、上肢执⾏、技能沉淀和开放部
署，⼀⽬希望让机器⼈学会的本领能够留下来，让更多客⼾和开发者参与创造操作技能。正如
其所说：“上肢是我们的，场景是客⼾的，⽣态是所有⼈的。”
再把⽬光放远⼀些。今天，机器⼈可以帮助⼈类完成⼯⼚⾥的精密装配、危险操作；未来，它
们或许还能深⼊⼈类难以抵达的地⽅，替我们完成更多探索。
在⼀⽬科技创始⼈李智强的设想⾥，未来，⼈类⾝处地球，机器⼈却能在三亿公⾥外的⼩⾏星
上独⽴完成采样，接过原本需要⼀个团队协作完成的⼯作。
「让⼈类突破⾏动的边界」。遥远的星空⾥，就这样伸出了⼀只来⾃地球的⼿。

### Page 6

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX tăng điểm trong tuần qua, +9.57 điểm tương ứng +0.55%, với thanh khoản suy 
giảm. Tập đoàn công nghiệp, Tiện ích Điện và Bất động sản là các nhóm ngành có mức tăng 
rõ rệt nhất trong tuần qua. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với 
giá trị -810 tỷ đồng, tập trung ở các mã FPT, VCB và STB; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều bán 
ròng ở mức -13 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng -1,161 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần qua 
và đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -12,049 hợp đồng. Tự doanh đảo chiều bán ròng -2,033 
hợp đồng và đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -6,103 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam trải qua tuần hồi phục nhẹ với biến động tăng/giảm đan xen, thanh khoản 
ở mức thấp và nhà đầu tư nước ngoài tiếp tục đẩy mạnh bán ròng. Hai phiên giữa tuần 
chứng kiến sự hồi phục mạnh mẽ của thị trường, phản ứng trước thông tin NHNN có động 
thái hỗ trợ thanh khoản hệ thống ngân hàng để hạ lãi suất thông qua nghiệp vụ hoán đổi 
ngoại tệ, và mua vào dự trữ ngoại hối. Dù vậy, áp lực bán nhanh chóng quay trở lại ở 2 
phiên cuối tuần khi nhà đầu tư hoài nghi về triển vọng hạ lãi suất trong ngắn hạn, trong 
khi TTCK toàn cầu cũng có diễn biến kém khả quan trước đà hồi phục của giá dầu, số liệu 
CPI Mỹ cao hơn kỳ vọng, và diễn biến tiêu cực ở nhóm cổ phiếu AI.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX tiếp tục hình thành thêm một mẫu nến spinning trên khung tuần, hàm ý tương 
quan cung cầu đang tạm thời cân bằng; trong khi diễn biến tăng nhẹ về điểm số nhưng đi 
kèm sự sụt giảm của thanh khoản cho thấy tín hiệu hồi phục mang tính phân hóa rõ nét. 
Trên khung ngày, nhịp bật tăng tích cực của chỉ số sau khi chạm đường biên dưới của mẫu 
hình tam giác đi ngang trung hạn đang cho dấu hiệu chững lại tại vùng kháng cự gần 
quanh 1.750, phản ánh lực cầu mặc dù được củng cố khá tốt ở vùng giá thấp nhưng thiếu 
sự đồng thuận khớp lên các vùng giá cao. Diễn biến giằng co có thể còn tiếp diễn, tuy 
nhiên khi xu hướng ngắn hạn vẫn đang nghiêng về chiều tăng, chỉ số được kỳ vọng sẽ tiếp 
tục hướng lên, thử thách lại các vùng cản phía trên. 
(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

2 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
The U.S. Capex Boom No One is Talking About—And Its Implications
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
We have long been of the belief that the U.S. is on the cusp of an inbound FDI capex boom
that can result in numerous positive spillover effects for the U.S. economy. The latest

figures from the U.S. Department of Commerce bear this out.
Exhibit 1 highlights the growing push among foreign multinationals to be inside the U.S.,
with FDI totaling nearly $240B in the first six months of this year. That is a staggering
103% jump from a year ago and places FDI inflows on track for a record year.
The latest inbound capex already adds to the massive scale of foreign investment in the
U.S. To wit, the accumulated stock of FDI in the U.S. stands at nearly $6T, based on the

latest figures from the Bureau of Economic Analysis (BEA). Manufacturing accounts for
roughly 43% of the total, and, at last count, some 10,000 foreign affiliates were operating
in the U.S.
These firms are largely invisible to the average U.S. investor, but the activities of U.S.
affiliates of foreign multinationals are substantial and competitive- and growth-enhancing.
To this point and based on the latest data from the BEA, foreign affiliates in the U.S.

employed nearly 8.6 million U.S. workers in 2024. That represents 6.1% of total private
sector employment, with manufacturing and retail the two most prominent sectors
of employment. Roughly 24% of all manufacturing jobs in the U.S. are from foreign
subsidiaries. In terms of pay, U.S. workers at these firms make $89,000 annually, 7% higher
than the national average, according to the Global Business Alliance.
Foreign affiliates also contributed some $1.5T to U.S. GDP; comprised some 15.7% of total

U.S. private sector investment; accounted for 12.4% of total U.S. research & development
(R&D) expenditures; paid roughly 20% of all U.S. federal taxes; and made up 22.5% of
U.S. exports and 26.2% of U.S. imports.1 These are the direct effects of U.S. subsidiaries
of foreign companies operating in the U.S. The indirect effects are even greater in terms
of more income, jobs, growth and tax revenue—all bolstering the long-term competitive
advantage of the U.S. In a nutshell, foreign firms operating in the U.S. are an important

ingredient of U.S. economic growth.
There are multiple factors behind surging inflows: One, due to rising U.S. trade
protectionism, foreign multinationals increasingly want to be “inside” the U.S. economy,
not outside. Companies that once served the U.S. market exclusively through exports
increasingly face higher costs and more uncertainties and hence want to be “inside.”
Two, with a $32T-plus economy with extraordinary consumer purchasing power, foreign

firms prefer to be closer to their customers via investment than just trade (exports). There
is no more dynamic consumer in the world than the American shopper.
Three, America’s massive investment cycle in AI, data centers, semiconductors, power
generation, etc., has created numerous investment opportunities for many foreign firms
that don’t want to miss out on America’s surging capex cycle.
Four, and finally, abundant energy supplies, deep capital markets and a highly developed

technological ecosystem have also induced more FDI inflows.
As more U.S. affiliates of foreign multinationals set up shop in the U.S., think more job
creation across the country, more manufacturing output, more locally sourced purchases,
more U.S. exports, more spending on R&D, and more tax revenue for local communities.
And over the long run, think a more globally competitive U.S. economy.
1 Activities of U.S. Affiliates of Foreign Multinational Enterprises, 2024” and “Activities of U.S. Affiliates of Foreign

Multinational Enterprises, 2023”, BEA July 2026.
Investment Implications
The boom in foreign investment in
the U.S. should continue to support
employment, productivity, corporate
earnings, and broader U.S. economic
competitiveness, in our view. Hence
our U.S. bias.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
And speaking of global competitiveness, Exhibit 2A is very illustrative of the magnet pull of
the U.S. economy to attract FDI versus the rest of the world. Of new greenfield investment
this decade, note the overriding preference for the U.S., accounting for nearly 16% of total
global greenfield investment this decade. America’s share is larger than the next three
countries combined—the United Kingdom, India, and Germany. And note that America’s

share of new investment is more than 5X that of China’s. Most of this new investment
has gone into manufacturing sectors like electrical equipment, computers, appliances and
batteries and has originated from Asia and Europe.
The bulk of foreign investment comes from Europe, followed by Japan and Canada, but as
Exhibit 2B highlights, there is a new dynamic playing out when it comes to America’s ability
to attract capital. There is a new source in town: a handful of developing nations (namely

Taiwan, South Korea, Mexico and Brazil). Mexican manufacturers, Taiwan technology
leaders, South Korea chip leaders, Brazil industrial firms, India pharmaceutical firms—these
companies and more are realizing that in today’s trade environment, the best way to serve
the American market is not necessarily through exports but through investment—investing
locally. FDI flows from developing nations reached $63B in the 12 months through June,
more than double the level a year prior ($24.5B), according to the BEA. Fueled by insatiable

AI demand, flows from South Korea alone totaled $23.4B in the first half of 2026. The
upshot is that an increasingly important source of capital for the U.S. is coming from an
unlikely source: the developing nations.
More fire for the capex boom. The coming boom in FDI inflows is supportive of our thesis
that the U.S. stands on the cusp of a major industrial upgrade. We are in the early innings
of a structural—not cyclical—capex cycle that should lead to the rebuilding of domestic

supply chains, higher value-added manufacturing, and more AI-driven productivity growth.
Foreign firms, in our opinion, will be part of this process.
That is important to recognize because remember the following: when a foreign company
builds a factory or an R&D facility or expands its existing manufacturing capacity right
here in the U.S., good things happen. Demand for U.S. goods and services goes up (as does
demand for construction, materials, logistics and professional services). New jobs are created,

boosting incomes of U.S. workers, while supporting more local spending and consumption.
And because many foreign firms often bring advanced manufacturing technologies when
investing in the U.S., the end results include a boost in productivity and innovation in various
sectors of the economy.
All the above, of course, are the secret ingredients to stronger U.S. economic growth, rising
corporate earnings and higher equity prices. With a little help from our friends, America’s

industrial renewal is gaining strength and momentum.

MARKET VIEW
Europe’s Electoral Cycle and Market Risks
Ehiwario Efeyini, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
As midterm elections approach for the U.S. next month, voters across Europe will also
enter their own election season starting this November and continuing throughout
2027. Over the next year, 52% of the total EU population, representing 46% of EU GDP
across eight countries, will go to the polls, including voters in three of its four largest

economies (Exhibit 3). Having announced snap general elections earlier this month,
Spain will vote for members of its lower and upper house on November 29. A series of
parliamentary elections are then due for 2027 in Estonia (by March 7), Finland (on April
18), Greece (by July 25), Slovakia (by September 28), Poland (by November 11) and Italy
(by December 22). And perhaps most consequentially France will stage Presidential
elections on April 18, with a potential second round of voting set for May 2.

For the EU as a whole, the significance of these coming elections will be the potential
for a shift toward more populist, domestically oriented policy priorities from leading
opposition candidates (particularly in France, Spain and Poland) which could obstruct the
process of closer EU integration. In the nearer term, adoption of the new seven-year
(2028-2034) EU budget due by the end of 2027 could be impeded. And over the longer
term, renewed efforts toward an EU-wide capital market union to deepen and attract

more inward investment into European markets could also be further delayed.
At the country level, investors have seen bond yields rise globally in 2026 on the back of
higher inflation and concerns over fiscal sustainability. The debt question has arguably
been most pressing in parts of Europe, especially given the likelihood that upcoming
elections make for domestic increases in government largesse. And of the eight
countries going to the polls over the next year, France remains the biggest source of

concern. Interest rate spreads on French 10-year sovereign bonds have overtaken those
in both Greece and Italy this year (Exhibit 4), driven by investor concerns over public
debt. The 2027 government budget unveiled earlier this month projected a deficit of
5.0% of GDP (well in excess of the 3% specified in the EU Stability and Growth Pact),
but remained insufficiently expansive to nullify the risk that the current government
could be unseated later this year if it is forced through the fragmented parliament. This

comes on top of an existing debt load of close to 120% of GDP and a halving of official
2026 growth forecasts from 1.0% at the start of the year to 0.5% in September. In
short, all four variables in France’s debt sustainability equation currently stand as major
headwinds, and the two leading candidates in the 2027 election have shown limited
appetite for fiscal consolidation over the coming years.
Exhibit 3: Europe's Upcoming Elections Represent Around Half of EU GDP and Population.

Investment Implications
We remain cautious on European
markets overall, despite seeing
pockets of strength in selected
areas such as defense, data center
infrastructure and renewable energy.
On top of growing uncertainty
from the upcoming electoral cycle

and interest rate outlook, rising
competition and slowing demand
from China continue to challenge
key segments of the Consumer
Discretionary sector. And limited
direct AI concentration also leaves
Europe under-exposed to the key
growth theme that continues to buoy
equity markets in the U.S., Japan and
emerging Asia.

Intervention from the ECB under its 2022 bond purchase program is also unlikely at this

stage given that the spread widening in France has not appeared either disorderly or
unwarranted. Meanwhile, conditions could potentially be aggravated by expected ECB rate
hikes in response to still above-target inflation across the eurozone. Indeed recent moves
to tighten monetary policy from a number of major central banks have been read most
hawkishly in Europe. Most of the increase in European bond yields has come from higher
inflation breakevens as opposed to real yields in the U.S. and Japan. And despite some

paring back of tightening expectations in the wake of recent bond volatility, the market is
still pricing three more policy rate increases over the next 12 months.
Euro depreciation and an emerging shift toward more protectionist trade policy with China
could further complicate the ECB policy outlook. China posted a record $35B trade surplus,
according to Eurostat, with the EU in September (alongside its $938B total surplus for the
first three quarters of 2026), and the two markets have agreed to reduce the EU bilateral

deficit by capping imports of hybrid cars for four years. Hybrids account for less than 2%
of total EU imports from China, but this could potentially mark the start of a broader shift
toward more trade protection in Europe with its largest foreign supplier which risks adding
further upward pressure to cost-push inflation. And though the leading candidates in the
French election may favor resuming oil and gas imports from Russia, Europe also remains

among the most at risk from the global energy shock. Indeed some 56% of the region’s
crude oil consumption is in the form of diesel product according to the International Energy
Agency, more than twice the U.S. share.
The 2- to 10-year eurozone yield curve has already flattened significantly since the start
of the year. And at just over 30 basis points as of mid-October, it remains flatter than
its equivalents in the U.S., UK and Japan. Outside Energy and Technology, Financials have

been among the strongest-performing sectors in Europe this year. But additional ECB
rate hikes could lead to further curve flattening, compressing bank net interest margins
and contributing to slower growth—particularly given that rate increases are coming in
response to supply-driven inflation rather than reflecting stronger economic activity.
We nonetheless continue to see pockets of strength in European markets. Growing
defense budgets should remain a tailwind for Industrials as national governments look to

meet their North Atlantic Treaty Organization targets over the next several years. And the
sector should be further supported by indirect exposure to AI investment through electric
cabling, power infrastructure and building materials for data centers. Leaders in renewable
energy equipment and services are also likely to be beneficiaries of grid upgrades and
broadening electrification globally, particularly given elevated fossil fuel prices. But our view

on the region remains cautious overall. On top of growing uncertainty from the upcoming
electoral cycle and interest rate outlook, rising competition and slowing demand from
China continue to challenge key segments of Europe’s Consumer Discretionary sector.
Limited direct AI concentration in technology hardware, cloud services and application
software also leaves Europe underexposed to the key growth theme that continues to buoy
equity markets in the U.S., Japan and emerging Asia.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
Cautious Optimism Entering Fiscal Year 2027
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
“A renewed focus on rebuilding fiscal space is urgent, given elevated debt, higher
borrowing costs, multiplying demands on public budgets and heightened uncertainty.” So
warned the IMF about the global fiscal outlook last week in its updated World Economic

Outlook report. Much is at stake for developed nations in Europe and Asia facing high
existing debt, unfavorable demographics and more modest growth.
For the U.S., between structurally higher mandatory outlays, the administration’s $1.5T
proposed defense package, and the 10-year U.S. Treasury yield sitting above 5%, spending
is likely headed higher. We continue to fade concerns of an impending U.S. debt crisis,
however, largely because economic growth continues to surprise to the upside. Expanding

output is good news for the debt-to-GDP ratio, which remains a touch below 100%.
Indeed, rather than being led higher on account of un-anchored inflation expectations, the
10-year U.S. Treasury yield has repriced to reflect better-than-expected nominal growth
(Exhibit 5A). Higher yields in the face of slowing growth, meanwhile, would be a different
story, not only for the fiscal outlook (worse) but also for U.S. equity valuations (lower).
Of course, after the U.S. accumulated another $2T deficit in FY 2026,2 “rebuilding fiscal

space” in the IMF’s words will be politically unpopular and mathematically challenging.
In our view, the biggest near-term tail risk would be a surprise move lower in growth as
budget deficits at 6% of GDP in good times leave little room for bad times. This isn’t our
base case given robust consumer spending per our internal credit and debit card data and
ongoing investment in the AI buildout and beyond. We expect these two pillars to sustain
growth in 2027.

The other good news is that, contrary to concerns, demand for Treasurys remains robust.
Yields are attractive—that was the message from October’s successful $39B 10-year
Treasury note auction. At the same time, U.S. debt boasts an array of buyers: domestic
households, the Federal Reserve, mutual funds and exchange-traded funds, money market
funds, pension funds, foreign central banks, private investors overseas, and more. Per
Exhibit 5B, whereas foreign official investors once dominated some 40% of the U.S.

Treasury market during the 2008/2009 Great Financial Crisis, their share has fallen to
12% while domestic private investors now account for a majority of holdings. Translated:
demand is more diverse than most appreciate.
Exhibit 5: Higher Yields Meet Shifting Composition of U.S. Treasury Demand.
A) Higher 10-year Yield Consistent with Strong Nominal Growth Environment. B) Domestic Private Investors Now Dominate U.S. Treasury Market.

Investment Implications
In our view, higher nominal and real
yields present a compelling entry point
for investors seeking income and can
provide cushion in the face of higher
rate volatility. We expect demand for
U.S. assets to remain healthy and favor
the U.S. over developed economies in
Europe facing more modest growth and
fiscal instability.

volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: risks mount
By Ryan Wells, Elliot Clarke & Illiana Jain
10:31 November 20 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
Australia’s labour market disappointed again in October, employment falling 43.5k following
September’s 36.4k decline. On a three-month average basis, job gains over the year have now slowed
to 1.1%yr, materially below current growth in the working-age population, 1.7%yr. As a result, the

unemployment rate rose to a new post-pandemic high of 4.9% despite the participation rate falling
another 0.2ppts to 66.6%.

### Page 2

While employment growth’s descent initially reflected softer momentum in hiring, now we are seeing
evidence of a material increase in redundancies. Most affected workers look to have remained in
employment to date, but persistent job shedding raises the risk of a faster and more persistent uptrend
in the unemployment rate, potentially above our baseline peak forecast of 5.2% (at mid-2027) and most
certainly before the late-2028 peak of 5.1% the RBA expects.

As such, the robust 1.1% (3.5%yr) rise in the Q3 Wage Price Index warrants close scrutiny. The Q3
increase was skewed towards those on awards and enterprise arrangements – pay outcomes directly
tied to the Fair Work Commission’s decision and collective bargaining. Individual arrangements
meanwhile, the most responsive to current and expected labour market conditions, accounted for their
smallest share of a Q3 wage increase since 2019. Business survey detail also suggests labour cost

growth will normalise quickly after the award increase.
Deteriorating economic conditions are also increasingly evident in housing. The Westpac Housing
Sentiment Index fell 2.2% to 83.2 in November, among the weakest readings since 2005. Interestingly,
there remains considerable latent demand: almost 13% of consumers intend to purchase a home over
the next year, and relatively few plan to sell. For now, though, weak sentiment, high interest rates and

expectations of further price declines are keeping would-be buyers on the sidelines. These factors also
pose risks to the broader growth story.
In the US, abstracting from the Anthropic IPO, the outlook for inflation remained the critical theme. The
October FOMC minutes highlighted that inflation is currently being supported by tariffs and energy
prices as well as the AI buildout, with "several [members] judg[ing] that price pressures were
increasingly extending beyond industries directly affected by data center construction”. Participants

"assessed that medium- and longer-term inflation expectations continued to be consistent with the
Committee's 2 percent objective", although persistent above-target inflation is a threat to the status
quo, and perceived risks are skewed to the upside. FOMC members who spoke throughout the week
also kept the focus on price risks, and called for the FOMC to act swiftly. Governor Waller again
proposed a 50bp hike in December; others seemingly favour a 25bp hike at the December and January

meetings, as Westpac forecasts, and continuing to tighten further in 2027 if necessary.
Very clearly, the labour market and activity growth are not of concern for US policy makers. Retail sales
gave further justification for this view this week, the control group surprising to the upside with a 0.6%
gain in October after a material upward revision to September (0.5%). That said, housing is under
considerable pressure, pending home sales falling 17.1% over the past year, and housing starts back

at 2019 levels despite a 9.9% rebound in October. Persistent weakness in this sector will, in time, have
implications for both consumption and investment.
China’s October data round was the other key international development of the week. Again, it provided
a call for pro-active stimulus in scale. In the month, annual retail sales growth almost stalled at 0.4%yr
and the decline in fixed asset investment broadened outside property, with capital expenditure now

down 8.1% across the economy year-to-date. Industrial production again highlighted that its output to
satiate global demand which is holding up aggregate growth; though, even here, momentum softened
in October, annual growth in production slowing from 5.5%yr to 4.8%yr. It is not only activity that

### Page 3

Related articles
remains under pressure, but also wealth, with new / existing home prices 0.15% / 0.24% lower in the
month. As we continue to emphasise, to prove effective, stimulus needs to affect change in both activity
and wealth and do so in a sustainable manner.

Document 306: Construction rotates from infrastructure to housing and data centres (2026-11-28)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Construction rotates from infrastructure to housing and data centres (2026-11-28).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-28
Pages: 18
Status: ok

### Page 1

#australia #construction
Construction rotates from infrastructure to housing and data
centres
By Pat Bustamante
Senior Economist, Westpac
14:36 November 25 2026 Share
Construction activity rose 0.9%qtr in Q3 and 5.8%yr. Construction activity remained solid,
driven by residential building (+3.1%qtr), non-residential building (+1.5%qtr, includes data
centres), partly offset by engineering construction (-0.9%qtr).

### Page 2

Construction activity rose 0.9%qtr in Q3 2026, taking annual growth to a strong 5.8%. The quarterly
increase was close to Westpac Economics’ 0.8%qtr forecast, but well above the 0.4%qtr market
consensus. Importantly, sizeable upward revisions to private construction lifted annual growth by
almost 2ppts. These revisions suggest the economy was somewhat stronger in 2025–26 than
previously thought, despite the same observed inflation outcomes.

What does this mean for the near-term outlook?
Overall, today’s data show construction activity continuing to expand at a solid pace, although there are
important offsetting forces beneath the headline result. Public infrastructure work remains soft and is
expected to ease further as major east-coast projects reach completion. This is being partly offset by
public non-residential construction, particularly health, schools and other social infrastructure.
At the same time, private construction remains strong. Residential construction is up 9.3% over the
year, the commercial building pipeline, which includes data centres, is around record highs, and the
renewable-energy investment pipeline remains substantial as we work towards the 82% renewable
electricity target by 2030.
Taken together, demand in the construction sector is likely to remain elevated in the near term, but its

### Page 3

composition is shifting. The pullback in public infrastructure is freeing up labour and other construction
capacity for housing, data centres, renewables and commercial projects. This rebalancing has
already been substantial, with our estimates suggesting the government footprint in the
construction industry declined by around 1.5ppts in 2025–26 (Government’s footprint plateaus as
services rise and construction retreats).
What does this mean for the National Accounts?

Today’s release is the first of a set of partials which will feed into next week’s September quarter 2026
National Accounts. Today’s numbers alone point to stronger growth in residential and new buildings
activity over the quarter and some upside for our Q3 estimate. However, with several important partial
indicators still to come, we will provide our full assessment in Friday’s GDP preview.
What about inflationary pressures and the impact of the data centre boom?

The spike in construction costs during the pandemic was largely synchronised across dwellings, non-
residential buildings and engineering construction. Today, however, cost pressures differ as demand
profiles across these segments diverge.
While rebounding in the quarter, engineering construction costs have been softer as major public
infrastructure programs wind down. In contrast, dwelling construction costs are accelerating as activity
in this segment picks up. Non-residential building costs appear to be going sideways, rather than

significantly reaccelerating, despite the strength in private investment, including data centres.

### Page 4

At 4.5%yr, aggregate construction cost growth is above what was recorded over 2018-19. This raises
questions about whether the strong demand in pockets like data centre investment are contributing to
broader inflationary pressures. For now, there is limited evidence of this happening. However,
construction cost deflators continue to firm in year-ended terms, suggesting that the disinflation
anticipated by the RBA may take longer to materialise.
How this plays out going forward depends on how much the substantial roll-off in public construction

activity makes room for the pickup in private residential and non-residential building work, particularly
data centres, and in private engineering construction associated with the renewable energy transition.
Total construction work yet to be done has risen to around 9.2% of nominal GDP in Q2 2026, compared
with an average of around 8.8% since 2022.
If that pipeline continues to build faster than public infrastructure releases capacity,
construction-sector cost pressures could prove more persistent than currently anticipated.

However, this is not our central case, as higher interest rates and ongoing cost increases are
likely to dampen private demand, while the public infrastructure pipeline continues to wind
down.

### Page 5

The detail
Residential construction outperformed, rising 3.1%qtr in Q3, following an upwardly revised 2.8%qtr
increase in Q2 2026 (previously estimated at 1.7%qtr). Growth was driven by a 3.9%qtr increase in
private residential construction, its strongest quarterly gain since June 2005, outside the COVID period.
In year-ended terms, residential construction is up 9.3%yr, the strongest growth rate since the
September quarter of 2016. This suggests the sector is responding to growing housing demand by

expanding output despite ongoing cost pressures. Some of this strength may also reflect the pullback
in public construction activity, which is freeing up labour and other resources for residential and non-
residential projects.
The strength in residential construction suggests the sector is responding more meaningfully to
Australia's housing shortage. However, with approvals likely to soften as higher interest rates bite and
demand moderates, the current pace of expansion may prove difficult to sustain.

### Page 6

Engineering construction (EC) declined 0.9%qtr in Q3 2026 but was 1.3%higher in year-ended
terms.
As foreshadowed in this month’s Market Outlook, the public infrastructure pipeline appears to have
passed its peak, as major transport projects across Australia’s east coast, particularly in NSW and
Victoria, move towards completion. Consistent with this, public engineering construction declined
3.0%year in Q3 but stabilised over the quarter to post a gain of 2.9%qtr. More broadly, the trend

remains weak, with activity falling in five of the past seven quarters.

### Page 7

In contrast, private engineering construction remains robust, up almost 5% over the year. However,
activity fell 3.7%qtr in Q3, suggesting some moderation may also be emerging in this segment. One
area of continued strength is electricity infrastructure, with the value of private work on electricity
generation and distribution rising by an estimated 10%qtr in seasonally adjusted and nominal terms,
largely reflecting renewable energy projects such as wind and solar developments.

Looking ahead, the private engineering pipeline remains supported by structural investment themes,
particularly the energy transition and nationwide rollout of data centres. However, constraints around
utility provision, grid connections and supporting infrastructure remain a key risk to the timely delivery
of these projects.
Non-residential building construction increased 1.5%qtr in Q3 2026, leaving activity 10.2% higher
over the year. Private-sector activity was mixed, declining 0.4%qtr, but remained 11% higher in year-

ended terms. Public activity also picked up sharply, rising 6.2%qtr to be 8.3% higher over the year,
partly reflecting investment in health and other facilities required to service a growing population.
Overall, non-residential construction remains strong, supported by two key investment themes: the
rollout of data centres in the private sector and increased public investment in health and other social
infrastructure, including in Queensland.

### Page 8

Although the contribution from individual projects is difficult to quantify, the surge in data centre
commencements during the second half of 2025 is likely supporting activity and should remain an
important source of construction demand over the second half of this year and beyond.

### Page 9

State detail
Construction activity increased across four of the six states in Q3, although outcomes varied
considerably.
South Australia recorded the strongest quarterly increase, rising 3.9%qtr, followed by NSW (+2.2%qtr)
and Victoria (+0.8%qtr). In contrast, activity declined in Western Australia (–1.9%qtr) and Queensland
(–1.2%qtr).
Despite the quarterly volatility, construction activity remained higher than a year ago across all states.
Tasmania recorded the strongest annual growth (+17.3%yr), followed by South Australia (+11.3%yr),

NSW (+8.1%yr), Western Australia (+6.4%yr), Victoria (+4.4%yr) and Queensland (+2.6%yr).
